# Today Habit tracker (App Store ID 1055295863) — Full Review Analysis

**Neybox Digital Ltd. · `com.neybox.Today` · "For to-dos, routines & goals"**

Every one of the **1,785 reviews** in `reviews.jsonl` was read individually, in its
original language, and hand-coded. Nothing was sampled, keyword-filtered or skipped.
Corpus: **2016-03-09 → 2026-07-13** (10.3 years), **70 storefronts**,
**18 languages**, corpus mean **3.97★**.

---

## How to read this

- Every quantified claim carries **count, percentage, denominator, scope, period, signal
  band and review IDs**. Signal bands are those in `GENERALIZED_STORE_REVIEW_ANALYSIS_PROMPT.md`.
- Theme counts are **non-exclusive**: a review carrying six ideas is counted in six themes.
  Percentages therefore sum past 100% and are *shares of reviews mentioning a thing*, never
  shares of a fixed pie.
- **§10 is a complete per-review index.** Any number here resolves to exact reviews; any
  review resolves to the findings it supports.
- Review IDs are given as `` `1345853521` `` (the `review_id` in `reviews.jsonl`) and as
  `#1` (position in date order, 1…1,785). Both appear in §10.
- Quotations are verbatim and short. Non-English text is quoted in the original with a
  translation. Store HTML entities (`&#39;`) are as they appear in the source.

### ⚠️ Ten warnings before any number here is used

1. **This corpus is not the app's rating.** The corpus mean is **3.97★**. Every public
   storefront mean is **4.5–4.8★** (§E3 external). Written reviews here skew far more
   negative than silent star taps. Do not present 3.97★ as "the app's rating".
2. **The corpus is front-loaded to 2016.** 645 of the 1,785 reviews are from
   2016, and **45.32% of the corpus falls in era E1** (Mar 2016 – Apr 2017) alone. Any
   global percentage is therefore dominated by how the app behaved **ten years ago**. Part 7
   exists because the global numbers, on their own, are actively misleading.
3. **Reviews decayed to near zero.** 59.1 reviews/month in E1 → **1.7/month** in E5.
   Late-era percentages rest on denominators of 59 (E5) and 13 (E6). They are
   directional only and are labelled as such.
4. **A review is not a user.** There are 1,785 reviews from 1,785 distinct `review_id`s
   and **zero repeat authors** within a storefront, but nothing links a person across
   storefronts or to an install.
5. **60 reviews are edited** (`is_edited`). The store shows only the final text; earlier
   versions are unrecoverable. Where a reviewer narrates their own edit, that is used.
6. **Selection bias runs both ways.** People review after delight and after breakage. The
   2016 launch wave and the 2022 black-screen wave are both bursts of motivated writers.
7. **Only 254 reviews carry any helpfulness votes**, and the maximum is 82. Vote
   counts are a weak popularity signal here, not a validity signal.
8. **Storefront is not language and not nationality.** 22 reviews on the China
   storefront are in English; see §6.6.
9. **No paid-user population is knowable.** 188 reviewers state they paid. That is a
   **self-selected 10.53% of reviewers**, not a conversion rate. No conversion,
   revenue or download figure is derivable from review text and none is claimed here.
10. **Six of the 70 storefronts clear the 50-review bar.** The other
    64 contribute to every global number but get no standalone claims (§6.8).

---

# PART 0 — READ THIS FIRST

The eight findings that should change a decision.

## 0.1 The product's differentiator is visual, and it worked for ten years

`PR_DESIGN` is the single largest theme in the corpus: **460 reviews, 25.77%**,
High-priority signal, mean **4.33★**, 2016-03-09 → 2026-03-22. Add the per-habit
cover photo (`PR_PHOTOS` 97, 5.43%) and the visual union reaches
**523 reviews, 29.30%**, mean 4.36★.

Reviewers do not treat this as decoration. They repeatedly explain it as the *mechanism*:

> "I'm a very visual person, so the combination of large pictures and colourful
> calendars/graphs is great. I really feel motivated to tick things off, more than any
> other habit app." — #1137, `2337983734`, gb, 4★

> "the ability to create custom backgrounds for each different habit!" — named as the one
> reason productivity improved most across 15 trackers in three years — #551, `1478570559`, gb, 5★

> "It sets a craving, when I see the image, I want to accomplish the goal." — #1188, `2636687666`, us, 5★

Paired with simplicity (`PR_SIMPLE` 361, 20.22%, mean 4.68★) this is
what "best in category" rests on: `PR_BEST` 204 reviews (11.43%), mean
**4.78★** — the highest mean of any large theme.

**Evidence:** `3581019264`, `3610997192`, `2867064627`, `1696422920`, `1887236677`, `1838939733`, `2337983734`, `1916508512`, `4761141223`, `9052716705`.

## 0.2 The second differentiator — "cards" — is loved by a minority and ignored by most

The per-habit dashboard of cards (counters, custom charts, milestones, journal, photo
gallery, Apple Health) is what reviewers name when they explain why they chose Today over
a prettier-but-thinner rival. `PR_CARDS` 70 (3.92%), mean **4.69★**;
the depth union `U:depth_differentiator` reaches 192 (10.76%),
mean **4.72★**.

> "決め手は他のに比べてカードによる付随情報の拡張性です" (*the deciding factor was the
> extensibility of supplementary information via cards*) — #683, `1524523819`, jp, 5★, who
> was running Today alongside Momentum and Habity and paid for this one

But the same feature draws the corpus's most specific *negative*: `NEG_FEATURE_UNUSED`
15 reviews (0.84%), mean 2.80★ — people
saying a headline feature does nothing for them.

> "cards that are unusable in 95% cases" — #1344, `3644769024`, ua, 3★
> "those cards aren't really needed" — #550, `1478423070`, in, 5★
> "Fotos für jede Gewohnheit → ist mehr eine Spielerei für den Anfang" (*photos per habit
> are more of a gimmick at first*) — #667, `1520730080`, at, 4★

**This is the central product tension.** The cards are simultaneously the reason power
users pay and a source of confusion for everyone else (`UX_CONFUSE` 74,
4.15%; reviewers who cannot work out the custom chart, the counter or the
milestone card: #794, #1651).

**Evidence for cards:** `1700023106`, `2053507045`, `2054180552`, `1392329202`, `1767675198`, `6892875164`, `1613185608`, `1808438163`. **Against:** `1365035315`, `1587170340`, `1591724160`, `2026175358`, `2268497498`, `5294830409`, `1357805646`, `1478423070`.

## 0.3 Reliability, not features, is what destroyed this product

`U:bug_any` covers **469 reviews (26.27%)**, High-priority signal, mean **2.89★**.
The narrower "it does not work" union `U:reliability` (crash, won't launch, lag, glitch,
broken by update) is **234 (13.11%)**, mean **2.71★**.

The era table (§7) is unambiguous. Bug mentions rise **12.36% → 50.85%**
of reviews from E1 to E5 while praise falls **77.87% → 33.9%**.
The corpus mean falls monotonically across five consecutive eras:
**4.16 → 4.14 → 3.59 → 3.51 → 3.07**.

The largest single defect families:

| Family | n | % of 1,785 | Band | Mean ★ |
|---|---:|---:|---|---:|
| `BUG_CRASH` | 126 | 7.06% | High-priority signal | 2.71 |
| `BUG_WATCH` | 88 | 4.93% | Very strong signal | 2.98 |
| `BUG_PERF` | 70 | 3.92% | Very strong signal | 2.99 |
| `BUG_NOTIF` | 63 | 3.53% | Very strong signal | 2.57 |
| `BUG_LAUNCH` | 58 | 3.25% | Very strong signal | 2.26 |
| `BUG_SWIPE` | 39 | 2.18% | Meaningful signal | 3.95 |
| `BUG_WIDGET` | 35 | 1.96% | Meaningful signal | 3.20 |
| `BUG_SYNC` | 32 | 1.79% | Meaningful signal | 2.53 |

**Evidence:** `5541959561`, `5953889344`, `4921815578`, `1611850727`, `2433135123`, `3259733326`, `8046860191`, `1606690337`, `3924163363`, `4253855988`.

## 0.4 A performance bug that scales with success

The most damaging defect in the corpus is one that only appears **once a user has succeeded**:
the app slows in proportion to accumulated check-ins.

`BUG_PERF` is 70 reviews (3.92%), rising from 0.74% of E1
to **16.95% of E5**. Reviewers quantify it precisely:

> "it takes about 5 seconds to register, if I'm registering a long streak of 50 day's or so,
> **which is how I've been using it for 3 years**, it takes so long I've stopped bothering."
> — #1382, `3827656315`, gb, 1★

> "チェックイン数が上がるほどに動作が重くなっていき、1,000日以上になってからは一回タップしたら
> 謎のフリーズ→アプリ落ちる" (*the more check-ins accumulate the heavier it gets; past 1,000 days
> a single tap means a mysterious freeze then a crash*) — #1716, `9295872427`, jp, 1★

> "das sind doch keine wirklich großen Datenmengen, wenn man etwas 200mal abgehakt hat,
> sondern **die Speicherung muss ineffizient programmiert sein**" (*200 check-ins is not
> really a large amount of data; the storage must be inefficiently programmed*)
> — #1756, `11596711598`, de, 3★

> "It can take over a minute from when you mark something as done, until it actually gets
> checked. I've contacted support multiple times over the past 6 months" — #1694, `8580429365`, us, 1★

This is the churn engine. A habit tracker that punishes long streaks with latency drives
away exactly the users who proved the product works. Of the 70 reviewers reporting it,
17 also record leaving, being blocked, or threatening to leave.

**Evidence:** `4921815578`, `1611850727`, `2433135123`, `8046860191`, `1606690337`, `3924163363`, `4253855988`, `1527351492`, `1776951942`, `1831745786`.

## 0.5 The Apple Watch app was requested for 14 months, shipped, and never worked properly

`REQ_WATCH` runs from 2016-03-11 — 34 requests (1.90%). The app
shipped around May 2017. From that moment `BUG_WATCH` becomes one of the largest defect
themes in the corpus: **88 reviews (4.93%), Very strong signal, mean 2.98★**,
against only 21 reviews that praise it (1.18%).

The complaints are consistent across eight years: check-ins revert, the phone and watch
disagree, and the complication is unavailable on most watch faces.

> "often I go back later in the day only to find out that several 'check ins' I did earlier
> had reverted to 'not done'" — #854, `1620769515`, us, 2★
> "I tap an icon to say I've done it, then 20 minutes late it's unchecked again!!" — #1015, `1920258131`, gb, 1★
> "there is only one watch face that'll work with this app, and it's NOT my chosen watch face"
> — #1655, `7720157586`, us, 4★

Requests for Infograph complications (Series 4, 2018) recur for **seven years** and are
never answered: #1559, #1264, #1473, #1267, #1318, #1655, #1669, #1727, #1266, #1268.

**Evidence:** `5953889344`, `2056463243`, `3259733326`, `1606690337`, `1613185608`, `2040841545`, `4995905641`, `1610282924`, `2268497498`, `3280194125`.

## 0.6 The free tier went 1 → 3 → 5 habits, then back to 1 — and the complaint came back with it

This is the clearest natural experiment in the corpus, and the dates are unambiguous.

| Free tier | Complaint code | n | First | Last | Reviews 2017–2020 |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---:|
| **1 habit** | `MON_FREE1` | 78 | 2016-03-10 | 2024-09-30 | **0** |
| **3 habits** | `MON_FREE3` | 67 | 2016-03-22 | 2022-05-13 | — |
| **5 habits** | `MON_FREE5` | 1 | 2017-11-13 | 2017-11-13 | — |

⚠️ **The 5-habit stage rests on a single review** (#1014, `1918053513`, Nov 2017: "免费版可以设5个
习惯，对一般人来说，够用了" — *the free version allows 5 habits, enough for most people*). The 1→3
change and the return to 1 are both supported by dozens of reviews; the intermediate 3→5 step
is a single observation and is treated as such everywhere in this report.

At launch the free tier allowed **one** habit. 71 reviews complained about it
in 2016 alone; it is the dominant theme of the first fortnight. On ~22 March 2016 the
developer raised it to three, and reviewers noticed **within a day**:

> "I gave it 1 star because I hated the fact that they only gave you 1 habit… Fast forward a
> month and all the BAD REVIEWS AND ANGRY Customers they had a change of heart and allowed now
> three habits to track.
> Just because of this I re downloaded the app and **because they actually listened to there
> customers I just paid full price for it**!" — #169, `1351843491`, us, 5★

Then, between **January 2017 and December 2020 — four full years — not one review complains
that the free tier allows only one habit** (0 reviews). The complaint
returns in 2022:

> "you can only track ONE habit with the free version and can't add any cards… If they had
> just advertised this as a paid app, it would have been fair." — #1690, `8311997753`, us, 2★
> "no puedo agregar más de 1 hábito, tengo entendido que en la versión gratuita puedes agregar
> máx. 3" (*I can't add more than 1 habit; I understood the free version allowed up to 3*)
> — #1691, `8362191635`, pe, 1★

**The size of the effect, stated carefully.** Reviews complaining about a **one**-habit free
tier carry a mean of **1.97★** (78 reviews) — the lowest mean of any
monetisation code in the corpus. Reviews complaining about a **three**-habit free tier carry
**3.60★** (67 reviews). The same grievance, attached to a tier three
times wider, arrives with a rating **1.63 stars higher**.

That is a difference between two groups of complainers, not a measured causal effect of the
change — the two groups are separated by six years and a different app. But the direction is
corroborated by the four-year silence in between and by reviewers narrating the change
themselves (#169, #1000, #175). Narrowing the tier back to one habit in ~2022 re-imported
the 2016 complaint, in the same words, from the same kind of reviewer.

**Evidence, 1-habit era:** `1346837988`, `1346902450`, `1345853521`, `1345892687`, `1345901751`, `1345960703`, `1345966768`, `1345971169`, `1346019622`, `1346036611`.
**Evidence, return of the 1-habit tier:** `9591308969`, `10949219510`, `8222194432`, `8311997753`, `8362191635`, `9159842495`, `11781486186`.

## 0.7 Pricing: a one-time $4.99 unlock was an asset; it is now a $299.99 tier

One-time pricing is **actively praised**: `PR_ONETIME` 23 reviews (1.29%),
mean **4.61★**. The defence union `U:pro_pricing` (one-time praise + "worth it" +
defending the price + paying to support the developer) reaches 124 reviews
(6.95%) at mean **4.75★**.

> "the biggest selling point of this app is that it offers a lot of functionality that
> otherwise would have been locked behind a subscription. Paying once (4.99) for all these
> features is the prime reason I use this app as a Habit Tracker compared to others."
> — #863, `1626756462`, gb, 5★

> "I can't believe some apps out there have a yearly subscription circa £30… this one is a
> steal at £5" — #931, `1708111245`, gb, 5★

**As of 13 September 2026 the $4.99 one-time unlock no longer exists.** The US listing shows
three in-app purchases (§E2, external):

| In-app purchase | Price |
|---|---|
| All premium features | **$299.99** |
| Today Premium Annual | $9.99 |
| Today Premium Monthly | $3.99 |

Reviewers found this before I did, and their arithmetic is public:

> "why is Today Premium $10 per year, but ALL Premium Features are $300 ❓ … when will THIS
> or ANY App be worth $300 ❓" — #1704, `8946229677`, us, 2★
> "Hubo un tiempo que la versión premium estaba $4.99 y ahora no lo pude conseguir a ese
> precio. Actualmente esta $299.99" (*premium used to be $4.99; now I can't get it at that
> price — it's currently $299.99*) — #1693, `8448197948`, gt, 2★
> "I paid for this app and reviewed it years ago (when it was under a different pricing
> model)… for the $300 you're asking for *now*" — #1781, `13865667618`, us, 5★

`MON_SUBSCRIPTION` is 8 reviews, **all from 2022-07-21 onward**,
mean 2.62★. Every one is negative or anxious. The corpus contains **no
review that welcomes the subscription**.

## 0.8 The app stopped shipping for 1,030 days — starting with a build that would not launch

This is the finding that external data nails down (§E4). Apple's version history shows
**1,030 days between 1.9.63 (2022-11-09) and 1.10 (2025-09-04)**, and **zero releases in
2023 or 2024**.

Version 1.9.63 shipped on **9 November 2022**. The very first black-screen review is dated
**9 November 2022**:

> "2022.11.09版本更新之后无法打开程序" (*after the 2022.11.09 version update the app won't
> open*) — #1712, `9271340335`, cn, 3★

Eleven more follow within six weeks — #1712, #1713, #1714, #1715, #1716, #1717, #1718, #1719, #1720, #1721, #1724 —
and then nothing was shipped to fix it for **two years and ten months**.

`DEV_STALE` — reviewers saying the app is abandoned — is 67 reviews
(3.75%), Very strong signal, mean 3.01★, and **20.34% of all
E5 reviews**. Users audited the developer themselves:

> "Likely abandoned: * intro video doesn't work… * no update in 2 years * copyright dates on
> web page only thru 2021 * **email bounces because the embedded address is toda[sic]@neybox.com**
> … (Odd because sister app Pillow is working well, updated, etc.)" — #1770, `12543093674`, us, 1★

> "团队是准备转移重心到pillow了吗，有点可惜" (*is the team shifting focus to Pillow? a bit of
> a pity*) — #1476, `5060119494`, cn, 4★

**The app is now being updated again** — 11 releases between Sep 2025 and Feb 2026 (§E4) —
but E6 contains only 13 reviews, far too few to judge the recovery.

# PART 1 — SCOPE AND METHOD

## 1.1 Files used

| File | Role | Records |
|---|---|---:|
| `app store review/86. Today Habit tracker.../reviews.jsonl` | **the corpus**; every claim resolves here | 1,785 |
| `by_country/*.jsonl` | 70 per-storefront files | 1,785 (union) |
| `manifest.json` | extraction metadata, per-country counts, rating distribution | — |
| `_state.json` | collection state for 118 polled storefronts | — |

## 1.2 Schema actually present

All 13 fields are present on all 1,785 records; none is null or empty.

| Field | Used for | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `review_id` | primary key | 1,785 distinct; **zero duplicates** |
| `country`, `country_name` | §6 | 70 storefronts |
| `rating` | §4 | integers 1–5 only |
| `title`, `body` | **read in full for every record** | no empty titles, no empty bodies |
| `author` | authenticity checks only (§9.H) | **zero repeat (country, author) pairs** |
| `date` | §7 | ISO-8601 UTC, all well-formed |
| `vote_count`, `vote_sum` | §9.H | 254 reviews have any votes; max 82 |
| `is_edited` | §9.H | 60 edited reviews |
| `app_id`, `app_name` | constant (`1055295863`, `Today Habit tracker`) | — |

**Not present, and therefore never claimed:** app version per review, device, developer
responses, install date, purchase state, country of residence, or any user identifier that
survives across storefronts.

## 1.3 Coverage and reconciliation

`86-reconcile.py` compares the four sources. Everything agrees exactly:

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Records in `reviews.jsonl` | **1,785** |
| Distinct `review_id` | **1,785** — zero duplicates |
| Identical `(country, author, title, body)` tuples | **0** |
| Union of 70 `by_country/*.jsonl` files | **1,785** — exact match, both directions |
| Per-record byte equality, country file vs main | **1,785/1,785** identical |
| `manifest.total_reviews` | 1,785 — matches |
| `manifest.storefronts_with_reviews` | 70 — matches |
| `manifest.reviews_per_country` | matches for all 70 storefronts |
| `manifest.rating_distribution` | matches exactly |
| `manifest.mean_rating` (3.971) | computed 3.97 — matches |
| `_state.json` storefronts polled | 118 (48 returned zero reviews) |
| `_state.json` `sum(collected)` | 1,785 — matches |
| `_state.json` incomplete storefronts | **0** |
| Malformed dates | **0** |

**Reviews read: 1,785/1,785 = 100%.** Reviews hand-coded: 1,785/1,785 = 100%.
No deduplication was applied, because none was warranted.

## 1.4 Processing method

1. `86-dump.py` emits all 1,785 reviews in date order with a stable index (1…1,785).
2. Reviews were read in **18 batches of 100**, in order, in full, in the original language.
   Machine translation was used as a reading aid for zh/ja/ko/ru/tr/pl/bg only; every quoted
   translation in this report was checked against the source string.
3. Each review received hand codes plus a note quoting the text that drove them
   (`86-cls/*.txt` — 1,785 lines).
4. **After every batch**, `86-check-cls.py` re-asserted each line's `review_id` against the
   corpus at that index. This caught three ID-alignment drifts affecting 25 reviews, all
   corrected before proceeding (§9.C).
5. `86-build-classification.py` rejects unknown codes, folds two aliases, adds mechanical
   codes (`STAR_n`, `EDITED`, `VOTED`) from the record, and derives 25 unions.
6. `86-validate.py` runs **636 checks**; all pass (§9.C).
7. `86-aggregate.py` computes every number cited (8,300 facts) into `86-gen/_facts.json`.
8. `86-build-report.py` fills every placeholder in the report source from that file and
   **refuses to write if any placeholder is left unresolved**. No number and no review ID in
   this report was typed by hand; each is substituted from the computed data at build time.
9. `86-verify-report.py` re-checks the written file against the corpus (§9.M).

**Classification is hand judgement, not an algorithm.** No clustering, keyword rule or model
assigned a code. The cost of that is that codes cannot be mechanically re-derived; the benefit
is that the note on every line in `86-cls/` quotes the evidence, so any code can be audited.

## 1.5 Signal thresholds

Applied to the global corpus (denominator 1,785) and separately to each eligible country
(denominator = that country's reviews).

| Share of reviews | Label |
|---|---|
| < 0.1% | Ignore by default |
| 0.1% – < 0.5% | Weak signal |
| 0.5% – < 1% | Emerging signal |
| 1% – < 3% | Meaningful signal |
| 3% – 5% | Very strong signal |
| > 5% | High-priority signal |

At n=1,785, 0.1% ≈ 2 reviews and 5% ≈ 89 reviews. Segment rates (within paid reviewers,
within a star band, within an era) are always labelled as such and always shown with both the
segment denominator and the global count.

## 1.6 Limitations, stated plainly

- **The corpus mean is not the app's rating.** 3.97★ here; 4.5–4.8★ publicly
  (§E3). The gap is ~0.6–0.8 stars. Written reviews are a biased, more negative sample.
- **Time dominates everything.** 45.32% of reviews are from E1. A global percentage
  is mostly a statement about 2016–17. Part 7 is not optional reading.
- **Late eras are thin.** E5 = 59 reviews over 34 months; E6 = 13 over 10
  months. Percentages there move by ~1.7 points per review. Treated as directional only.
- **No version field.** Releases are dated from Apple's public version history (§E4, back to
  Mar 2020 only) and, before that, from what reviewers say. Pre-2020 release dates in this
  report are **inferred from reviews** and labelled.
- **`lang` is my reading**, not detected. Judged from script and content.
- **Sentiment is not inferred from stars.** A 5★ review whose text is a pure bug report is
  coded as a bug and flagged `CONTRA_RATING` (46 reviews, §9.H).
- **No conversion, revenue, download or retention figure** is derivable here, and none is given.
- **Accessibility evidence is absent.** Two incidental mentions in 1,785 reviews. That is a
  hole in the evidence, not evidence that there is no problem.
- **Survivorship.** People who quietly stopped using the app in 2019 mostly did not write.
  The churn codes (98 reviews, 5.49%) capture only those who announced it.

---

# PART 2 — PRODUCT AND MONETIZATION MODEL

## 2.1 What the product is, from the reviews alone

Reconstructed from what reviewers describe using, not from the listing:

- **One full-screen page per habit**, each with a **cover photo** (stock gallery or your own).
  Swipe left/right between habits; swipe up for detail. This is the defining choice and the
  single most-discussed aspect of the app (§0.1, §3.2).
- **Check-in** by double-tap, by 3D Touch/force-touch, by swipe in the list, from the
  Notification-Centre widget, from a rich notification, or from the Watch.
- **Cards** attached per habit: streak calendar, custom counter, custom chart, milestones,
  journal/notes, photo gallery, Apple Health metrics (steps, weight, sleep, mindfulness,
  heart rate), and a "limbic"/stress card (added ~Jul 2018).
- **Scheduling**: daily; specific weekdays; N times per week (max 6) or month (max 25);
  multiple check-ins per day (added Sep 2016).
- **Reminders**: multiple per habit, rich/actionable notifications.
- **Badges and milestones** for streaks and totals.
- **Profile** screen with persistence statistics — which several reviewers found only by
  accident (#931, #1637).
- **Backup/restore** to iCloud Drive or Dropbox. **No account, no login, no server sync** —
  praised (#237) and blamed (#1303, #1369, #1181) in equal measure.
- **Privacy lock** via passcode / Touch ID / Face ID.
- **Apple Watch app + complication** (~May 2017).
- **Today/Notification-Centre widget** (Oct 2016); **removed Sep 2021, restored Nov 2021**.
- **Dark mode** (Oct 2021). **URL scheme / action URLs** (noted by #1154, #1250, #1506, #1521).
- **No advertising** at any point (14 reviews mention advertising at all; the
  substantive ones **praise its absence**, `PR_NOADS` 7).
- **No social, sharing, community or competition features** — and reviewers disagree about
  whether that is a flaw (§9.F).

**What reviewers say it is *not*:** a to-do list, a calendar, or a workout tracker. Several
draw the line explicitly — "Habits are different than to-dos and should be tracked
differently" (#938, `1720312452`), "I didn't want a workout tracker, just a yes/no - did you
hit the gym today?" (#224, `1359218702`), "it's not riddled with calendars and to do lists"
(#969, `1790008122`).

## 2.2 What the store listing says — external, 13 September 2026

Captured from the iTunes Lookup API and the US App Store page (§E1–E5). Kept separate from
review evidence.

| | |
|---|---|
| First released | **2016-03-08** |
| Current version | **1.18**, 2026-02-13 |
| Minimum OS | **iOS 18.0** / watchOS 11.0 |
| Category | Health & Fitness (secondary: Productivity) |
| Size | 290.4 MB |
| Localisations | 11: EN, FR, DE, IT, JA, KO, PT, RU, ZH-Hans, ES, TR |
| Seller | Neybox Digital Ltd. — also ships **Pillow** (sleep), named by reviewers in both praise and complaint |

The listing's own premium list: custom photo covers and premium galleries; unlimited cards
per habit; premium cards; unlimited habits; iCloud/Dropbox backup and restore; and the
Face ID / Touch ID / passcode lock.

## 2.3 Free / paid / trial / unclear — classified

| Capability | Status | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| Habit tracking, check-in, streak calendar | **Free**, but capped at **1 habit** today | listing + #1684, #1690, #1691, #1710, #1759 |
| Number of habits | **Paid** beyond the cap (cap has been 1 → 3 → 5 → 1 over ten years) | §0.6 |
| Custom photo covers / premium galleries | **Paid** — *and moved behind payment after being free*, taking existing covers with it | listing + #1734 |
| Cards beyond a small number | **Paid** | listing + #1690 |
| Premium cards (incl. the stress/"limbic" card) | **Paid** | listing + #1293, #1323, #1524 |
| iCloud Drive / Dropbox backup and restore | **Paid** | listing + #1219, #1156 |
| Touch ID / Face ID / passcode lock | **Paid** | listing + #66 (objects to this on principle) |
| Reminders, widget, Apple Watch app, Health integration | **Free** | no review reports them gated |
| Advertising | **None, ever** | §9.F |
| Trial | **None.** The free tier *is* the trial — the most-requested monetisation change in 2016 was a real one (`MON_TRIAL_REQ` 33, 1.85%) | §5.5 |

**Pricing history, as far as the evidence supports it:**

| Period | Price | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 2016 – ~2021 | **one-time unlock**, ~$4.99 / £3.99 / €4.99 / ¥30 / ¥600 / A$8 / CA$7 | 112 reviews name a price; §9.G |
| ~2022 onward | **subscription** added; one-time tier repriced | #1701 (Jul 2022) is the first to ask "Suscripción?" |
| 13 Sep 2026 (observed) | $3.99/mo, $9.99/yr, **$299.99 lifetime** | §E2 |

The transition window is bounded by reviews: #1651 (Aug 2021) still calls it "the small price
for the premium features"; #1701 (Jul 2022) asks about a subscription. The exact date is not
recoverable.

## 2.4 Data and privacy

Apple's privacy label (§E5) declares **no tracking**, no advertising identifier, and only
Identifiers/Usage/Diagnostics linked to identity for analytics and app functionality.

Reviews agree. `PR_PRIVACY` 4, `PR_NOADS` 7, `PR_SECURITY` 3;
against `PRIV_CONCERN` 4. The concerns that do exist are specific and fair:

> "What is the point of protecting your goals with password / touchid / faceid if **3D Touch
> widget reveals all my habits** even if I force-close the app?" — #1479, `5116388115`, ua, 4★
> "If the app keep opening on the background, **no password is required** to launch the app."
> — #1037, `2004605175`, us, 3★
> "指紋認証画面へ入る手前に一瞬、設定している習慣を見ることができる？" (*can you briefly see the
> configured habits just before the fingerprint screen?*) — #381, `1449566698`, jp, 5★

Three independent reports, across three languages and three years, that the privacy lock
leaks the habit list. Small counts, but the pattern is coherent and the feature is paid.

## 2.5 What could not be established

- Any download, revenue, conversion or retention figure.
- The exact dates of the free-tier changes (1→3 Mar 2016 is well-bounded by reviews; 3→5 and
  5→1 are not).
- The exact date the one-time unlock was withdrawn.
- Whether developer replies were ever posted on the App Store — the corpus has no reply field.
  Reviewers do report replies by email and Twitter, in both directions (`PR_DEV` 39,
  `SUP_NORESP` 25).

---

70 storefronts. Counts are non-exclusive.

**Praise and complaint are not separate populations.** 152 reviews
carry both a praise code and a bug code; 81 praise the design *and*
report a defect. This is the corpus's characteristic voice: people who like the app describing
what is broken in it.

## 3.2 What people value — positive themes in detail

### The visual model (high-priority)

`PR_DESIGN` 460 (25.77%) · `PR_PHOTOS` 97 (5.43%) ·
union 523 (29.30%), mean 4.36★.

The recurring argument is that beauty is *functional* for a habit app because it makes you
want to open it:

> "デザインが美しいのでモチベーションが上がりやすい（←意外と大事なポイント）" (*because the design is
> beautiful, motivation rises easily — a surprisingly important point*) — #1300, `3440039586`, jp, 4★
> "So beautiful that it makes me want to just do the items so I can click them off." — #995, `1863740117`, us, 5★
> "if it isn't all there in front of me in an endearing way, then I slack off and say 'NOPE'"
> — #1215, `2914912972`, ca, 5★
> "It changes habit tracking from a chore into a fun experience I enjoy and look forward to."
> — #453, `1458034202`, au, 5★

One reviewer's professional credential is worth recording:

> "As someone who worked in broadcast graphics design and even received the Good Design Award
> in Japan, I can say that Today is one of the most thoughtfully designed apps I have used."
> — #1782, `13875862512`, jp, 5★ (2026)

**IDs:** `1696422920`, `2053507045`, `2337983734`, `1392329202`, `2251254322`, `6531490642`, `8046860191`, `5209402359`, `1347226423`, `1347650463`.

### Simplicity (high-priority)

`PR_SIMPLE` 361 (20.22%), mean **4.68★**. Reviewers define it
negatively — as the absence of what rivals do:

> "No frills, no fuss—no need to sit through way-too-long animations and stories and
> celebrations every time you do something." — #1711, `9243354141`, us, 4★
> "打卡软件不需要太多花里胡哨的，这样就刚好" (*a check-in app doesn't need lots of frills; this is
> just right*) — #1001, `1881181884`, cn, 5★
> "It doesn't give you confetti or a cookie but it helps form that path in your brain" — #972, `1795611028`, us, 4★
> "Perfect app. Don't ruin it with clutter!" — #341, `1427635117`, us, 5★

**IDs:** `1700023106`, `2053507045`, `1838939733`, `1916508512`, `9052716705`, `1781266665`, `1718809802`, `2056463243`, `5927354285`, `6531490642`.

### Best-in-category, and the named competition

`PR_BEST` 204 (11.43%), mean **4.78★** — the highest of any theme
above 100 reviews. `CMP_NAMED` 48 (2.69%) reviews name a rival.

Products named by reviewers, with the year first named: **Way of Life** (2016), **Momentum**
(2016), **Coach.me** (2016), **Streaks** (2016), **Done** (2016), **Productive** (2016),
**HabitBull** (2017), **Habity** (2017), **Lark** (2016), **iBetter** (2018), **tiny routine**
(2018), **Strides** (2018), **Due** (2019), **Shanbay/扇贝** (2017), **Todoist**, **Day One**,
**Evernote**, **Headspace**, **Notion** (2022). Apple's own **Reminders**, **Calendar**,
**Health** and **Activity** are named as both the thing replaced and the fallback.

The corpus's most-voted review (38 up / 82 total) is a direct comparison:

> "I have no idea how the developers came along and blew **Momentum, Strides, and Way of Life**
> out of the water … but they did. I've been using Way of Life for nearly half a decade now…
> Momentum is too minimalistic (though I like their desktop sync, wink!) and Strides has a
> bubbly interface I can't stand." — #1326, `3581019264`, us, 5★, 2018-12-27

**IDs:** `3581019264`, `1700023106`, `1696422920`, `2053507045`, `1838939733`, `2337983734`, `4761141223`, `2054180552`, `1767675198`, `3029399400`.

### Motivation and outcomes

`PR_MOTIVATION` 184 (10.31%), mean **4.83★**.
`PR_OUTCOME` — a *concrete* result, not just encouragement — 106 (5.94%),
mean **4.84★**, the highest mean in the corpus.

> quitting smoking*) — #747, `1553250877`, de, 5★, a medical student
> "6年間ほど習慣化を継続できている" (*I've sustained habit-formation for about 6 years*) — #1778, `13496344540`, jp, 5★

One review deserves separate attention for what it says about the mechanism:

> "Ich habe mit dieser App begonnen, meine **Depressiven Phasen** besser in den Griff zu

> app to get a better grip on my depressive phases, and to sort days when I felt bad out of the
> streak. Over time it became easier to assess my successes*) — #762, `1562859639`, de, 5★

That user is describing the `REQ_SKIP` feature request (11 reviews) as a mental-health
tool. See §8.6.

## 3.3 What goes wrong — negative themes in detail

### Reliability (high-priority)

`U:reliability` 234 (13.11%), mean **2.71★**. Breakdown:
`BUG_CRASH` 126, `BUG_PERF` 70, `BUG_LAUNCH` 58,
`BUG_UPDATE` 42, `BUG_GLITCH` 26.

Five distinct crash waves are datable from the corpus (§7.3): launch (Mar 2016), iOS 9/10
(Oct–Nov 2016), Face ID / Touch ID (Mar 2018), iOS 12 / watchOS 5 (Sep 2018), and the
black-screen build (Nov 2022). `BUG_UPDATE` — "a specific update broke this" — is
42 reviews (2.35%), mean 2.55★.

**IDs:** `5953889344`, `3259733326`, `1386797586`, `1477444905`, `1877326969`, `1965717805`, `2026175358`, `2268497498`, `2296678211`, `2644074855`.

### Reminders that do not fire (high-priority)

`BUG_NOTIF` 63 (3.53%), mean **2.57★**, spanning
2016-04-04 → 2025-02-10 — **ten years, never fully fixed**. For a habit
app this is not a peripheral defect; several reviewers say so explicitly:

> "notifications are straight up not working for habits. I have 3 set up and **they never go
> off**." — #520, `1472871151`, us, 3★
> "The idea of the app is to have reminders so you develop habits. Reminders are extremely hit
> or miss." — #1640, `7333847009`, us, 2★
> "em dez hábitos, notifica um ou dois, e por dias nenhuma notificação é entregue" (*out of ten
> habits it notifies one or two, and for days no notification is delivered*) — #1766, `12293267990`, br, 3★
> "if I don't check in every single day, **the reminders stop all together**" — #1674, `7936221496`, us, 3★

That last one is a specific, reproducible behaviour reported independently in 2017 and 2021
(#933, #1674): missing a day silences the reminder — the opposite of what a habit app should do.

Of the 63 reviewers, 10 say it makes the app
unusable for them.

**IDs:** `1887236677`, `9052716705`, `1781266665`, `1610282924`, `1649142062`, `2253847908`, `2864021791`, `3332433489`, `3605717518`, `3821792514`, `1358586053`, `1416163455`.

### Data loss (very strong)

`U:dataloss_any` 55 (3.08%), mean **2.67★** —
`BUG_DATALOSS` 33, `BUG_BACKUP` 28, `BUG_ICLOUD` 17,
`BUG_SAVE` 12.

**Backup and restore is a paid feature**, which makes these the most serious complaints in the
corpus. 12 of these reviewers also state they paid.

> "this is the **third time** I've opened the app to find months worth of data has been randomly
> corrupted/lost. I've tried restoring data from iCloud, but it's all affected… **despite having
> paid extra to back it up**" — #1219, `2955539119`, us, 1★
> "I lost my **3 years** data due to an issue in restore backup" — #1553, `5866189908`, it, 1★
> "その肝心のバックアップが上書きされ復元データまで初期化されてしまいました。もうどうにもなりません"
> (*the crucial backup was overwritten and even the restore data was wiped. Nothing can be done
> now*) — #1079, `2168696471`, jp, 4★, titled さらばToday ("Farewell, Today")
> "iCloud明明开着却没有自动备份，数据全都没了" (*iCloud was clearly on but there was no automatic
> backup — all the data is gone*) — #1720, `9310883434`, cn, 1★
> "ESTOS SUJETOS HAN PERDIDO TOOOOOODA MI INFORMACIÓN EN 2 OCACIONES" (*these people have lost
> ALL my information on 2 occasions*) — #1592, `6432248651`, pe, 1★

`REQ_ACCOUNT` (3) is the request this generates: users asking for a login so
their history is not hostage to one device.

**IDs:** `6655422389`, `2056463243`, `2296678211`, `3172596237`, `3244408577`, `3332433489`, `3593920728`, `3644567378`, `3864511286`, `5995726707`, `7837241403`, `1345878357`.

### The navigation model people fight with

`BUG_SWIPE` 39 (2.18%) — horizontal swipe between habits conflicting
with vertical scroll — spans 2016-03-11 → 2024-08-11, reported in seven
languages, and is **the single longest-running unfixed complaint in the corpus**.

> "Swiping from habit to habit is frustrating because about half the time, instead of going
> sideways… it starts going up" — #69, `1346490678`, us, 2★ (2016)
> "The horizontal scrolling performance still, well, isn't performing! **Same same since summer
> last year at least.**" — #1089, `2229824122`, de, 4★ (2018)
> "das swipen zwischen den Bildern hängt immer noch und anscheinend **seit einem Jahr noch nicht
> verbessert**!" — #1345, `3646704940`, de, 3★ (2019)

Paired with `UX_FRICTION` 41 (2.30%) — too many taps to do a simple
thing — and `REQ_DASHBOARD` 70 (3.92%), the demand to see all
habits at once, this is one coherent complaint: **the one-page-per-habit model does not scale
past a handful of habits.**

> "Requiring each habit to be its own page makes this impractical for tracking more than a
> small number of items." — #629, `1501989659`, us, 2★
> "I have more than 30 habits to track and your UI looks more boring to scroll down.. please
> minimize that dashboard so i could see all my habits at once" — #1784, `14293434159`, lk, 2★ (2026)
> "as my list of habits gets longer (and my dashboards get more complex), it gets very slow and
> the list gets very difficult to navigate" — #1091, `2250385177`, za, 3★

`REQ_DASHBOARD` runs 2016-03-21 → 2026-07-13 — again, ten years.

**IDs:** `2867064627`, `1887236677`, `6655422389`, `1767675198`, `1781266665`, `2629913277`, `1797922613`, `2433135123`, `3659008632`, `3440039586`, `4562259234`, `7315695928`.

### Onboarding and comprehension

`UX_CONFUSE` 74 (4.15%) · `UX_ONBOARD` 20 (1.12%).
The recurring failures are specific: people cannot find check-in, cannot understand the
custom chart or counter, cannot tell a skipped day from a missed one, and cannot find the
statistics.

> "I never understood how to interact with it. Although I was able to create a card, **I could
> never figure out how to 'check in.'**" — #895, `1659445486`, us, 2★
> "I only stumbled on the charts in the profile section **by accident**!" — #931, `1708111245`, gb, 5★
> "I can not to figure out what different between **skipped** and not to check in" — #1532, `5692422621`, tw, 4★
> "Die Statistiken sind z. B. im Profil. Man kann die App nicht mit der Liste der Ziele starten…
> Darum habe ich sie gelöscht, **ansonsten hätte ich sie gekauft**" (*…so I deleted it; otherwise
> I'd have bought it*) — #1637, `7249989187`, ch, 1★

That last review is the cost stated plainly: a confused user who was willing to pay.

**IDs:** `1797922613`, `4253855988`, `4995905641`, `12493500366`, `1365035315`, `1444371843`, `1451667264`, `1508553171`, `1510081684`, `1591724160`.

## 3.4 Unmet needs — the standing request list

Requests that recur across eras, with span. These are the demands the product never answered.

| Request | Code | n | % | Band | First | Last | Status |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|
| A widget (NC, then iOS-14 home screen) | `REQ_WIDGET` | 47 | 2.63% | Meaningful signal | 2016-03-10 | 2025-12-15 | NC widget shipped 2016, lost 2021, restored 2021; **home-screen widget never shipped** |
| See all habits at once | `REQ_DASHBOARD` | 70 | 3.92% | Very strong signal | 2016-03-21 | 2026-07-13 | never shipped |
| Apple Watch / more complications | `REQ_WATCH` | 34 | 1.90% | Meaningful signal | 2016-03-11 | 2021-02-21 | app shipped 2017; Infograph complications never |
| Third-party / Shortcuts integration | `REQ_INTEGRATION` | 34 | 1.90% | Meaningful signal | 2016-03-16 | 2026-03-13 | URL scheme only |

| iPad version | `REQ_IPAD` | 27 | 1.51% | Meaningful signal | 2016-03-14 | 2026-03-19 | **never, in 10 years** |
| More / better cover photos | `REQ_IMAGES` | 23 | 1.29% | Meaningful signal | 2016-08-03 | 2022-08-19 | never |
| More card types | `REQ_CARDS` | 29 | 1.62% | Meaningful signal | 2016-03-15 | 2023-01-11 | never |

| Auto check-in from Health | `REQ_AUTOCHECK` | 11 | 0.62% | Emerging signal | 2017-11-21 | 2021-08-02 | never |
| Record a *missed* day explicitly | `REQ_FAILMARK` | 6 | 0.34% | Weak signal | 2017-04-24 | 2018-12-20 | never |

### Three requests that deserve separate attention

**1. Archive/pause a habit (11 + 11 skip = 21 reviews).**
The most-upvoted *request* in the corpus (22 up / 36 total):

> "能不能增加停止某项习惯的功能？有时候有些习惯已经形成或者没必要了，但是目前**只能删除，结果所有数据会
> 跟着没有**，非常可惜" (*could you add a way to stop a habit? Sometimes a habit is already formed
> or no longer needed, but currently you can only delete it, and all the data goes with it —
> a great pity*) — #1087, `2223367800`, cn, 5★

The design forces a user who *succeeded* to destroy their record of succeeding. Restated
independently across five years and four languages: #1087, #1423, #1011, #1144, #1163, #1167, #1506, #1532, #1540, #1563.

**2. Bad-habit tracking (15 reviews, Emerging signal).** The App Store
description says "**Quit bad habits** and stay on track" (§E1). The feature does not exist.
The most careful statement of the gap:

> "If I were tracking (as I am now) whether or not I've had *any* soda, a single check-in could
> indicate success in abstaining for the day. But **if I only want to *limit* my intake** to,
> say, two sodas a day, I can't both log each drink and show success for not going over the
> limit." — #1651, `7645092267`, us, 5★

> "dans la description on nous parle de perdre ses mauvaises habitudes : il nous faut des
> explications. **En quoi cette application s'intéressent à nos mauvaises habitudes?**" (*the
> description talks about losing bad habits: we need an explanation. In what way does this app
> concern itself with our bad habits?*) — #1559, `5953889344`, fr, 4★

This is a **marketing-to-product mismatch**, not merely a missing feature — and `USE_QUIT`
shows 16 reviewers are trying to use it that way anyway.

**3. A note per check-in (12 reviews).** Second-most-upvoted request (31 up / 47):

> "每天的打卡如果可以有留言的功能就好了…想在对应的时间点记录下来。这样可以**更准确的认识自己**" (*it
> would be great if each check-in could carry a note… to record it at that point in time. That
> way you can know yourself more accurately*) — #1331, `3610997192`, cn, 5★

## 3.5 Churn — who leaves and why

`U:churn_any` 98 (5.49%), mean **1.94★** —
`CHURN_DELETE` 40 (said they left), `CHURN_BLOCKED` 43 (a
defect stops them using it), `CHURN_THREAT` 13 (will leave unless), `CHURN_FORGET`
2.

What the leavers cite, in order:

| Cited cause | Reviews also carrying a churn code |
|---|---:|
| Reliability (`U:reliability`) | 32 |
| Paywall (`U:paywall_complaint`) | 17 |
| Notifications (`BUG_NOTIF`) | 15 |
| Watch (`BUG_WATCH`) | 22 |
| Performance (`BUG_PERF`) | 17 |
| Data loss (`U:dataloss_any`) | 14 |
| Abandonment (`DEV_STALE`) | 12 |

In 2016 people left over the paywall. From 2018 they leave over reliability. That shift is
the whole story of this product compressed into one table.

**IDs:** `2056463243`, `3259733326`, `1346837988`, `1606690337`, `3924163363`, `1347857081`, `1348547237`, `1610282924`, `1649142062`, `1656131507`, `3332433489`, `4495003885`.

## 3.6 Support

`SUP_ASK` 54 (3.03%) reviewers ask the developer a direct question *in a
review* — itself a signal that the in-app support path is not obvious. `SUP_NORESP`
25 (1.40%), mean **2.20★**, report being ignored.

Against that, `PR_DEV` 39 (2.18%), mean **4.59★** — and in the early
years the developer was visibly, unusually responsive:

> "I gave feedback on the app and was very happy to hear that they are already working to
> incorporate multiple ideas that I had given them! They reply very quickly and kindly"
> — #207, `1356064325`, gb, 5★ (2016)
> "之前反馈闪退，不仅得到了回复，而且**几天就把问题修正了**" (*I reported a crash before; not only did
> I get a reply, the problem was fixed within days*) — #1033, `1974586345`, cn, 5★ (2017)
> "Dev Team reacted quite quick - the last update has significantly improved the check in
> experience once again… **Changed my rating to 5 stars**" — #1310, `3493496861`, de, 5★ (2018)

`FIX_CONFIRM` — reviewers confirming a fix or a delivered request — is 33 reviews
(1.85%), mean **4.76★**. **21 of those
33 are from before 2019.** Responsiveness is not an abstract virtue here; it is
visibly the thing that used to convert angry reviewers into advocates, and it stopped.

By the end, the same channel is silent:

> "I've contacted support, the developers and founders of the company directly and **haven't
> heard back from anyone in 6 months +**" — #1746, `10764479431`, gb, 1★ (2023)
> "サポートに尋ねたところ、まず返事をいただくまで**3週間**がかかり、ついに回答が来たら1行だけの単純内容で
> 「現在、インポートはできません。」" (*when I asked support it took three weeks to get a reply, and
> the reply was a single line: "import is currently not possible"*) — #1368, `3744435175`, jp, 3★

**IDs, unanswered:** `3924163363`, `1877326969`, `3998748702`, `4495003885`, `4830614591`, `12543093674`, `1430182193`, `1485987506`, `1516915209`, `2439609081`.

# PART 4 — RATINGS ANALYSIS

## 4.1 The distribution

| Stars | Reviews | % of 1,785 |
|---|---:|---:|
| ★★★★★ | 975 | 54.62% |
| ★★★★ | 318 | 17.82% |
| ★★★ | 175 | 9.8% |
| ★★ | 100 | 5.6% |
| ★ | 217 | 12.16% |
| **4–5★** | **1,293** | **72.44%** |
| **1–2★** | **317** | **17.76%** |

Corpus mean **3.97★**. Matches `manifest.mean_rating` exactly.

**This distribution is a time series wearing a disguise.** By year:

| Year | Reviews | Mean | 5★ share | 1★ share |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 2016 | 645 | 4.04 | 59.2% | 14.0% |
| 2017 | 401 | 4.51 | 72.8% | 3.7% |
| 2018 | 281 | 3.79 | 46.6% | 12.1% |
| 2019 | 167 | 3.67 | 41.3% | 12.6% |
| 2020 | 119 | 3.61 | 33.6% | 16.0% |
| 2021 | 70 | 3.51 | 38.6% | 17.1% |
| 2022 | 40 | 3.08 | 22.5% | 20.0% |
| 2023 | 23 | 3.39 | 47.8% | 26.1% |
| 2024 | 15 | 3.20 | 33.3% | 33.3% |
| 2025 | 18 | 3.11 | 38.9% | 33.3% |
| 2026 | 6 | 3.33 | 33.3% | 16.7% |

5★ share falls from 4.04 mean in 2016 to 3.11 in 2025; the 1★
share triples. Any statement about "the ratings" that ignores this is wrong.

## 4.2 Themes by star band

Percentages are **of that star band**, not of the corpus.

| Theme | 1★ | 2★ | 3★ | 4★ | 5★ |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `PR_DESIGN` | 10.6% | 25.0% | 17.7% | 24.2% | 31.2% |
| `PR_SIMPLE` | 0.9% | 7.0% | 8.0% | 18.2% | 28.7% |
| `PR_BEST` | 0.0% | 3.0% | 1.7% | 9.4% | 17.2% |
| `PR_MOTIVATION` | 0.0% | 0.0% | 0.6% | 9.1% | 15.8% |
| `PR_OUTCOME` | 0.5% | 0.0% | 0.6% | 3.5% | 9.5% |
| `PR_CARDS` | 0.5% | 0.0% | 1.1% | 4.4% | 5.4% |
| `U:bug_any` | 60.4% | 56.0% | 57.7% | 30.5% | 8.6% |
| `U:reliability` | 38.7% | 26.0% | 23.4% | 12.6% | 4.4% |
| `BUG_WATCH` | 8.3% | 14.0% | 13.7% | 5.0% | 1.6% |
| `BUG_NOTIF` | 8.3% | 12.0% | 9.1% | 4.1% | 0.4% |
| `U:paywall_complaint` | 31.3% | 26.0% | 15.4% | 11.0% | 4.1% |
| `MON_PAID_EXPLICIT` | 16.1% | 16.0% | 9.7% | 7.9% | 9.7% |
| `DEV_STALE` | 7.8% | 7.0% | 8.0% | 5.0% | 1.3% |
| `U:request_any` | 10.1% | 24.0% | 34.3% | 47.5% | 19.3% |
| `U:churn_any` | 22.1% | 22.0% | 9.1% | 3.1% | 0.2% |
| *(denominator: reviews in that star band)* | *217* | *100* | *175* | *318* | *975* |

Read across the rows:

- **`PR_DESIGN` appears in every band** — 10.6% of 1★ reviews still praise the
  design. Beauty does not protect the rating; it is what makes the failure worth writing about.
- **`U:reliability` is the strongest gradient in the table**: 38.71% of 1★
  reviews versus 4.41% of 5★. This is the rating-determining theme.
- **`MON_PAID_EXPLICIT` is roughly flat across bands** (16.13% of 1★,
  9.74% of 5★). Paying does not make people kind; see §5.
- **`U:paywall_complaint` is concentrated at the bottom** (31.34% of
  1★) but is not absent from the top (4.1% of 5★) — reviewers who
  love the app and object to the gate.

## 4.3 Five-star reviews (975, 54.62%)

Dominated by design (31.18%), simplicity (28.72%), motivation
(15.79%) and best-in-category (17.23%).

But **84 five-star reviews (8.62% of the band) also report a
defect** — the app's characteristic review is affectionate and bug-ridden at once. And
40 five-star reviews contain **no praise at all**, only a complaint (§9.H).

**IDs:** `3581019264`, `3610997192`, `1696422920`, `1838939733`, `1916508512`, `2629913277`, `6892875164`, `5927354285`, `5994362153`, `6531490642`.

## 4.4 Four-star reviews (318, 17.82%)

The "almost" band. Its signature is a request, not a fault: `U:request_any` reaches
47.48% here, its highest of any band. The classic four-star shape is
praise + one named missing thing, most often the Watch, a widget, an iPad version or an
overview screen.

`RATING_LEVERAGE` — reviewers explicitly withholding a star until something ships — is
16 reviews (0.90%) and is concentrated in 3–4★:

> "I'll be sure to change my rating to 5 stars when the updates come out!" — #1232, `3018036105`, gb, 4★
> "アップデートで対応していただければ、☆を増やします" (*if you handle it in an update I'll raise the
> stars*) — #984, `1819603242`, jp, 3★
> "Würde der App eigentlich 5 Sterne geben, aber dieser Umstand zwingt mich dazu 3 abzuziehen"
> (*I'd actually give the app 5 stars, but this circumstance forces me to deduct 3*) — #1082, `2186159961`, de, 2★

**IDs:** `1887236677`, `2337983734`, `4761141223`, `5953889344`, `1392329202`, `1781266665`, `5440947726`, `8070024063`, `2251254322`, `2433135123`.

## 4.5 Three-star reviews (175, 9.8%)

The most diagnostic band: people who still use the app and have stopped defending it.
`U:bug_any` 57.71%, `DEV_STALE` 8%, `U:ux_any`
14.29%.

> "Good app but I don't think it is supported anymore." — #1522, `5642646849`, us, 4★
> "Mixed bag pros/cons" — #1769, `12493500366`, us, 3★
> "Great, when working" — #1674, `7936221496`, us, 3★

**IDs:** `9052716705`, `1380036224`, `3998748702`, `5275952092`, `2030205717`, `3646704940`, `5135343553`, `5283905727`, `5483295228`, `8921268123`.

## 4.6 One- and two-star reviews (317, 17.76%)

| Theme in 1–2★ | 1★ | 2★ |
|---|---:|---:|
| `U:reliability` | 38.71% | 26% |
| `U:paywall_complaint` | 31.34% | 26% |
| `BUG_LAUNCH` | 11.98% | 10% |
| `BUG_NOTIF` | 8.29% | 12% |
| `U:churn_any` | 22.12% | 22% |
| `U:dataloss_any` | 8.29% | 9% |
| `MON_PAID_EXPLICIT` | 16.13% | 16% |

**The bottom band has two distinct populations, separated by time.** In 2016 a 1★ review is
almost always a paywall objection from someone who never got to use the app. From 2018 a 1★
review is almost always a long-term user whose app broke. The second kind is far more costly:
those reviewers had already paid.

2016-style:
> "Free version is worthless. You can only have 1 habit." — #28, `1346034110`, us, 1★

2022-style:
> "It has become increasingly slow with every update. Now it's unusable… I've contacted support
> multiple times over the past 6 months and nothing has changed." — #1694, `8580429365`, us, 1★

**IDs, 1★:** `1606690337`, `3924163363`, `1346015418`, `2026175358`, `2268497498`, `3821792514`, `4495003885`, `5369763057`, `1345892096`, `1345892603`.

## 4.7 Rating-versus-text contradictions

`CONTRA_RATING`: **46 reviews (2.58%)**, Meaningful signal,
where the star rating does not match the text. The asymmetry is severe:

| Direction | n |
|---|---:|
| **5★ whose text is only a complaint** | **40** |
| 1–2★ whose text is substantially positive | 4 |

Examples of 5★ reviews that are pure bug reports:

> 5★ — "Blank Screen after IOS update" (entire text) — #1719, `9309917073`, us
> 5★ — "widget在ios10下有bug / 经常出现紫色块" (*the widget has a bug on iOS 10; purple blocks keep
> appearing*) — #736, `1548898250`, cn
> 5★ — "sometimes a bit lag" (entire text) — #842, `1611850727`, hk
> 5★ — "La actualización no funciona! Entras en la App y se cierra sin que puedas hacer nada!"
> — #539, `1476921895`, es
> 5★ — "还好，但为什么超过三个要老子给钱" (*it's OK, but why do I have to pay to go past three?*) — #468, `1460920237`, cn

**Consequence for anyone reading the public rating:** the app's 4.5–4.8★ public average
(§E3) is inflated relative to written sentiment not only by silent star-tappers but by a
measurable population of users who write a complaint and tap five stars anyway. The true
signal is in the text, which is why every finding here is text-derived.

**All 46 IDs:** `1443163529`, `1456265513`, `1460920237`, `1473110350`, `1476921895`, `1477444905`, `1500084980`, `1527743362`, `1548898250`, `1552817117`, `1579178174`, `1593873286`, `1605939986`, `1611850727`, `1726591835`, `1781172805`, `2114107012`, `2272555834`, `2274068869`, `2274140308`, `2277264694`, `2655548751`, `2899369101`, `3021011228`, `3244408577`, `3374483677`, `3376482671`, `3517800749`, `3612195421`, `3622991803`, `3730175832`, `4039105951`, `4053683394`, `4130547456`, `4232022305`, `4830614591`, `4921815578`, `6880708561`, `7499355233`, `7801499738`, `7895168833`, `8224675831`, `9121599473`, `9309917073`, `9466256434`, `13101621990`.

# PART 5 — PAID-USER AND UPGRADE ANALYSIS

## 5.1 The population, and what it is not

**188 reviewers (10.53% of 1,785) state that they paid** — "I bought the
full version", "已购", "有料版を使っています", "j'ai acheté la version premium", "compré la versión
premium". Mean rating **3.69★** against a corpus mean of 3.97★.

**This is not a conversion rate.** It is the share of *reviewers* who volunteered that they
paid. Reviewers are a biased sample of users, and mentioning payment is itself selective —
people tend to mention it when defending a purchase or when the purchase failed them. No
conversion, revenue or paying-user figure is derivable from this corpus and none is claimed.

`MON_WILLBUY` — reviewers stating an intention or a condition to buy — adds 43
(2.41%). Together the two are 231 reviews touching purchase
intent directly.

## 5.2 Segment rates: what paid reviewers talk about

Denominator: the **188 self-identified paid reviewers**. Global % shown alongside so
the lift is visible. These are *segment rates*, not corpus signal bands.

| Theme | n in paid segment | % of 188 paid | % of all 1,785 | Lift |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `PR_DESIGN` | 39 | 20.7% | 25.8% | 0.80× |
| `PR_SIMPLE` | 25 | 13.3% | 20.2% | 0.66× |
| `PR_BEST` | 16 | 8.5% | 11.4% | 0.74× |
| `PR_WORTH` | 36 | 19.1% | 4.1% | 4.68× |
| `PR_ONETIME` | 9 | 4.8% | 1.3% | 3.71× |
| `PR_CARDS` | 14 | 7.5% | 3.9% | 1.90× |
| `PR_OUTCOME` | 16 | 8.5% | 5.9% | 1.43× |
| `U:bug_any` | 65 | 34.6% | 26.3% | 1.32× |
| `U:reliability` | 34 | 18.1% | 13.1% | 1.38× |
| `BUG_WATCH` | 8 | 4.3% | 4.9% | 0.86× |
| `BUG_NOTIF` | 9 | 4.8% | 3.5% | 1.36× |
| `U:dataloss_any` | 12 | 6.4% | 3.1% | 2.07× |
| `MON_RESTORE` | 16 | 8.5% | 1.0% | 8.43× |
| `MON_REGRET` | 11 | 5.8% | 0.7% | 8.73× |
| `MON_REFUND` | 6 | 3.2% | 0.4% | 8.18× |
| `DEV_STALE` | 9 | 4.8% | 3.8% | 1.28× |
| `U:churn_any` | 15 | 8.0% | 5.5% | 1.45× |
| `REQ_WIDGET` | 5 | 2.7% | 2.6% | 1.01× |
| `REQ_DASHBOARD` | 11 | 5.8% | 3.9% | 1.49× |
| `MON_EXPENSIVE` | 6 | 3.2% | 3.3% | 0.96× |

Three things stand out.

**1. Paid reviewers report defects at a higher rate than the corpus does.** `U:bug_any`
34.57% of paid reviewers versus 26.27% globally. That is expected —
they use the app more, for longer, with more habits, which is exactly the condition under
which this app degrades (§0.4).

**2. They carry the data-loss complaint disproportionately.** Backup is a paid feature;
12 of the 55 data-loss reviewers had
paid. Selling backup and then losing data is the corpus's sharpest breach of trust.

**3. They are the ones who defend the pricing.** `PR_ONETIME`, `PR_WORTH` and `MON_DEFEND`
all run far above their global rates inside this segment.

## 5.3 What triggers a purchase

`MON_TRIGGER` — reviews naming the specific thing that caused them to buy — 18
reviews (1.01%). What they name:

| Trigger | Example |
|---|---|
| **More habits** (the cap) | "習慣を増やしたかったので、有料版を使っています" (*I wanted more habits, so I use the paid version*) — #424, `1454413050`, jp |
| **Custom cover photos** | "I purchased the full thing **just because** to put my own photo covers" — #572, `1483615798`, us |
| **The cards / motivation boards** | "The addition of cards which let you create little motivation boards **was what made me pay** for the full version" — #104, `1347650463`, gb |
| **The developer listening** | "because they actually listened to there customers **I just paid full price** for it!" — #169, `1351843491`, us |
| **The Watch app** | "I bought this app **for the watch incorporation**" — #1025, `1951004228`, ca |
| **One specific premium card** | "I just bought the premium version **to calculate my stress level**" — #1323, `3555120788`, de |
| **Supporting the developer** | `MON_SUPPORT_DEV` 8 reviews — "I bought the full version **to support an app I use often**" — #242, `1364442019`, us |

The pattern: **people buy to remove the habit cap, and they buy for the visual/customisation
layer.** Nobody in this corpus reports buying for backup, for the passcode lock, or for
statistics — even though backup and the lock are premium features.

## 5.4 What goes wrong after purchase

This is where the corpus is most damaging, because these reviewers are the app's revenue.

**Purchase friction** — `U:purchase_friction` 51 reviews
(2.86%), mean **2.61★**:

| Failure | Code | n | Reading |
|---|---|---:|---|
| Purchase lost on reinstall / new device | `MON_RESTORE` | 18 | the largest single post-purchase failure |
| The IAP itself fails | `MON_IAP_FAIL` | 11 | includes a **March 2018 cluster** where the product showed as "no longer available" |
| Regret | `MON_REGRET` | 12 | — |
| Refund sought | `MON_REFUND` | 7 | two report Apple **refusing** it |
| Charged more than displayed | `MON_OVERCHARGE` | 2 | — |
| Paid feature later taken away | `MON_REGRESS` | 3 | — |

**`MON_RESTORE` (18 reviews, 2016-03-28 → 2021-02-12)**
is a nine-year unresolved defect that directly attacks paying customers:

> "我去年买了 卸载以后再用怎么要收费了？？？" (*I bought it last year — why does it charge again after
> reinstalling?*) — #928, `1698805807`, cn, 5★
> "I bought the premium features. Then I delete the app, now I install it again (on the same
> phone) and I have to buy the features again" — #1536, `5727531937`, nl, 4★
> "He comprado la apps y por error la he borrado. Y ahora al instalarla… me pide que vuelva a
> pagar" — #1618, `6868594425`, es, 1★, titled "¿Es una estafa?" (*Is it a scam?*)
> "I paid for the Premium **twice**" — #1779, `13517933118`, us, 2★

Note #1263 found the answer himself — Settings → Restore Purchases — which tells you the
function exists and is undiscoverable.

**The March 2018 purchase outage** deserves naming. 5 reviews that month,
mostly Chinese, report the in-app purchase itself reporting the item as unavailable:

> "每次付款都显示**该项目不再提供**，购买失效" (*every payment says this item is no longer available;
> the purchase fails*) — #1104, `2274068869`, cn, 5★
> "我往app store里充了50后和我说此项目不再提供？" (*I topped up 50 in the App Store and it tells me
> this item is no longer available?*) — #1116, `2284049297`, cn, 3★
> "I am trying to give you $4.99 I love the concept of the app but I can't buy the premium
> version. It keeps failing. **I wrote for help and no response.**" — #1174, `2568596703`, us, 1★

**IDs:** `1348119121`, `1569227742`, `1617412142`, `2096691014`, `2272555834`, `2274068869`, `2274140308`, `2284049297`, `2344516493`, `2568596703`, `3658763849`.

**Features taken back after purchase** — `MON_REGRESS` 3, small but severe:

> "Until they made it that **you have to pay to use your own pictures** and completely took
> everything i had on my profile off to do so" — #1734, `9814749006`, us, 1★ (Apr 2023)
> "サブスクが始まる前の買い切り版を購入している" (*I bought the one-time version from before the
> subscription started*) — #1783, `14015405307`, jp, 1★, now unable to manage habits at all

## 5.5 Upgrade barriers among non-buyers

`MON_WILLBUY` (43) is the most actionable monetisation code in the corpus: people
saying **exactly what would make them pay**.

| Condition stated | Example |
|---|---|
| Fix the Watch | "I will gladly pay for the full version but I really need it to work on my watch" — #872, `1637702429`, us |
| Ship a widget | "Les urgen widget y **les juro pago el premium**" (*they urgently need a widget and I swear I'd pay premium*) — #1749, `10949219510`, mx |
| Ship an iPad version | "Si la tuviesen para Ipad **la compraría**" — #1735, `9820894783`, es |
| Add every-N-days frequency | "If the app had this capability, I would rate it 5 stars **and purchase the full version**" — #608, `1494360640`, us |
| Add weekday-only reminders | "新增只工作日提醒。那么😊**我立马付费**" (*add weekday-only reminders and I'll pay immediately*) — #1160, `2492317967`, cn |
| Make the UI comprehensible | "Darum habe ich sie gelöscht, **ansonsten hätte ich sie gekauft**" (*so I deleted it, otherwise I'd have bought it*) — #1637, `7249989187`, ch |
| Fix the photo gallery | "**I won't update to premium** until the gallery is fixed" — #1510, `5535282962`, us |
| Just update the app | "I will gladly pay for an update if that is what is slowing the development down" — #1471, `4957079710`, us |

And the trial objection, which is the oldest and largest barrier of all — `MON_TRIAL_REQ`
33 (1.85%):

> "Expand the free app to allow a proper trial and then ask for money." — #26, `1346019622`, gb, 1★
> "unable to evaluate it with only one card… this should be listed as a paid app not free" — #73, `1346522458`, us, 1★
> "con uno no es suficiente para una prueba" (*one is not enough for a trial*) — #1749, `10949219510`, mx, 4★ (2024)

Eight years apart, the same sentence.

**IDs:** `1392329202`, `1389869620`, `1457410810`, `1591724160`, `4890658762`, `9591308969`, `10949219510`, `1365432840`, `1386500651`, `1458115387`, `1470536762`, `1473717022`.

## 5.6 What cannot be claimed here

- **No conversion rate.** 10.53% of *reviewers* said they paid. Nothing more.
- **No revenue, ARPU or LTV.**
- **No churn rate.** 98 reviews announce leaving; silent leavers are invisible.
- **No causal claim that the free-tier change drove purchases**, only that 18
  reviewers name a trigger and that the complaint disappeared for four years (§0.6).
- **No claim about the current subscription's performance.** It arrived in ~2022, into an era
  containing 59 + 13 reviews total. There is not enough evidence.

## 6.2 Eligibility

Six storefronts reach the 50-review threshold and get standalone analysis: **`ca`, `cn`, `de`, `gb`, `jp`, `us`**.
Together they are 1,269 reviews (71.09% of the corpus). The other
64 storefronts contribute to every global number and get no standalone
claims (§6.8).

## 6.3 United States — 595 reviews, mean 3.81★ — eligible

The largest storefront (33.33% of the corpus) and, at 3.81★, **the most
negative of the six eligible markets**.

| Theme | n | % of 595 | Band | vs global |
|---|---:|---:|---|---|
| `PR_DESIGN` | 154 | 25.88% | High-priority signal | ≈ |
| `U:bug_any` | 162 | 27.23% | High-priority signal | ≈ |
| `PR_SIMPLE` | 117 | 19.66% | High-priority signal | ≈ |
| `U:paywall_complaint` | 82 | 13.78% | High-priority signal | **above** (10.98%) |
| `PR_BEST` | 78 | 13.11% | High-priority signal | ≈ |
| `U:reliability` | 74 | 12.44% | High-priority signal | ≈ |
| `MON_PAID_EXPLICIT` | 60 | 10.08% | High-priority signal | ≈ |
| `MON_PRICE_NAMED` | 56 | 9.41% | High-priority signal | **above** (6.27%) |
| `PR_OUTCOME` | 51 | 8.57% | High-priority signal | **above** (5.94%) |
| `MON_FREE1` | 47 | 7.9% | High-priority signal | **above** (4.37%) |
| `U:churn_any` | 42 | 7.06% | High-priority signal | **above** (5.49%) |
| `BUG_WATCH` | 33 | 5.55% | High-priority signal | **above** (4.93%) |
| `REQ_DASHBOARD` | 25 | 4.2% | Very strong signal | **above** (3.92%) |
| `DEV_STALE` | 21 | 3.53% | Very strong signal | ≈ |

**Distinctively American:** price sensitivity expressed as *argument*. US reviewers name a
price (9.41%), reason about it, compare it to a cup of coffee, and
argue with each other about it in public — both directions.

> "$4.99, but what's that? just a cup of coffee? not much for being life-changing. I saw many
> reviews complaining about the 'cup of coffee' price tag. come on people, app devs gotta make
> a living." — #81, `1346840632`, us, 5★
> "In a market where most apps are $2.99 or less, only twice have I paid more than that:
> OmniFocus 2 and Martin's Index of Cocktails." — #32, `1346047627`, us, 2★

The US also supplies the highest rate of **concrete outcomes** (8.57%) and
the highest rate of **stated churn** (7.06%). It is the market that engages
hardest in both directions.

**IDs:** `8070024063`, `1347050529`, `1345853521`, `1345892687`, `1345901751`, `1345966768`, `1345971169`, `1346047627`, `1347226423`, `1348805097`.

## 6.4 China mainland — 353 reviews, mean 4.27★ — eligible

The second-largest storefront (19.78%) and, at 4.27★, **the most positive
of the six**. 341 of the corpus's reviews are in Chinese (19.1%);
22 reviews on the CN storefront are written in English.

| Theme | n | % of 353 | Band | vs global |
|---|---:|---:|---|---|
| `PR_SIMPLE` | 89 | 25.21% | High-priority signal | **above** (20.22%) |
| `PR_DESIGN` | 75 | 21.25% | High-priority signal | ≈ |
| `U:bug_any` | 87 | 24.65% | High-priority signal | ≈ |
| `U:reliability` | 47 | 13.31% | High-priority signal | ≈ |
| `MON_PAID_EXPLICIT` | 43 | 12.18% | High-priority signal | **above** (10.53%) |
| `U:paywall_complaint` | 29 | 8.22% | High-priority signal | ≈ |
| `MON_FREE3` | 19 | 5.38% | High-priority signal | **above** (3.75%) |
| `MON_RESTORE` | 8 | 2.27% | Meaningful signal | **above** (1.01%) |
| `LOC_TRANSLATE` | 6 | 1.7% | Meaningful signal | **above** (0.56%) |
| `PR_BEST` | 17 | 4.82% | Very strong signal | **below** (11.43%) |
| `U:churn_any` | 8 | 2.27% | Meaningful signal | **below** (5.49%) |
| `BUG_WATCH` | 9 | 2.55% | Meaningful signal | **below** (4.93%) |

**Four distinctively Chinese patterns**, each well-supported:

1. **Simplicity is valued above beauty** — the reverse of the global ordering.
   `PR_SIMPLE` 25.21% exceeds `PR_DESIGN` 21.25%. The recurring
   formulation is 简约不简单 ("simple but not simplistic"):
   > "简洁，没有多余功能，没有广告，是我用过习惯类应用中最好用的一个" (*concise, no superfluous
   > features, no ads, the most usable habit app I've used*) — #936, `1714907062`, 5★
   > "专注打卡，没有别的花哨功能，挺适合我" (*focused on check-in, no other frills, suits me*) — #590, `1487599628`, 5★

2. **Higher self-reported payment** (12.18% vs 10.53%
   globally), and a distinctive way of justifying it — framing the price as buying the developer
   a meal:
   > "30元当成请开发者吃顿饭又能怎样？" (*what's 30 yuan — think of it as buying the developer
   > dinner*) — #907, `1676747409`, 5★
   > "或许30是有点贵 可是完整地体验它 真的是美的享受" (*maybe 30 is a bit pricey, but experiencing it
   > fully is a beautiful pleasure*) — #896, `1659830676`, 5★

   And the counter-case, one of the most striking reviews in the corpus — a long, self-aware
   argument about affordability (`MON_AFFORD`, 3 reviews globally):
   > "为什么给4分，主要是因为**我穷**… 我犹豫了**一天零22个小时**，考虑到底要不要卸载… 10块钱以内我都能接受。
   > **开发app难道不是为了能让更多的人用么？**" (*Why 4 stars — mainly because I'm poor… I hesitated
   > for one day and 22 hours over whether to uninstall… under 10 yuan I could accept. Isn't the
   > point of making an app to let more people use it?*) — #892, `1656131507`, 4★

3. **Restore-purchase failures land hardest here** (2.27% vs
   1.01% globally) — 8 of the 18 global cases.

4. **Localisation is a live complaint**: `LOC_TRANSLATE` 6,
   `REQ_LOCALIZE` 4. The app gained Chinese in Sep 2016 and the translation
   was immediately criticised:
   > "check-in翻译成了什么鬼？不会翻译请添加语言选项" (*what the hell is check-in translated as? If you
   > can't translate, add a language option*) — #354, `1443163529`, 5★
   > "3d touch菜单是英文，widget显示的内容也没有汉化完整" — #553, `1479109039`, 4★
   > Traditional Chinese is still missing in 2018: "中文只有簡體是最大遺憾" — #1173, `2565901316`, tw, 4★

   One user wrote a step-by-step iOS Settings walkthrough *in a review* so other Chinese users
   could force the app into their language (#1121, `2293228457`).

**Note on `MON_FREE1` in China:** only 3 reviews — because the CN storefront
barely existed for this app in March 2016, when that complaint peaked. This is a
**timing artefact, not a cultural difference**, and §6.7 shows why.

**IDs:** `1796030878`, `1393768780`, `1656131507`, `1918053513`, `2296678211`, `3864511286`, `3959643144`, `1375169472`, `1375611755`, `1429671551`.

Germany is the **performance-complaint market**: `BUG_PERF` at 11.69% is over
three times the global rate, and German reviewers are the ones who diagnose rather than just
complain (#1756 on inefficient storage; #1089 tracking the same swipe bug across a year).
Conversely, price objections are *below* global — German reviewers largely accept the pricing
(7.79% vs 10.98%).

Germany also supplies the corpus's single most substantive account of the app as a
mental-health tool (#762) and one of its most detailed redesign critiques (#1310).

## 6.8 Japan — 76 reviews, mean 3.97★ — eligible

| Theme | n | % of 76 | Band | vs global |
|---|---:|---:|---|---|
| `U:bug_any` | 32 | 42.11% | High-priority signal | **far above** (26.27%) |
| `PR_DESIGN` | 19 | 25% | High-priority signal | ≈ |

**Japan has the highest defect-report rate of any eligible market** —
42.11% of JP reviews name a defect, against 26.27% globally — while
its mean rating (3.97★) is *above* the corpus mean. Japanese reviewers report bugs
carefully and rate generously. Any market-health read that uses the rating alone will
systematically under-weight Japanese defect signal.

They also write the corpus's most detailed feature requests. #381 (`1449566698`) is a
**twenty-item** structured request list covering bulk check-in, parent/child habits,
day-start time, font legibility on white wallpapers, Excel export, badge sounds, colour
coding, and a privacy leak — in 2016. Almost none of it was ever built.

> "アプリを買って以降、数ヶ月離れる時もあったが、再度戻ったりして**6年間ほど習慣化を継続できている**"
> (*since buying the app, though I've stepped away for months at a time, I keep coming back and
> have sustained habit-formation for about 6 years*) — #1778, `13496344540`, 5★

## 6.9 High-spend and high-review-volume markets

**Both groups are defined explicitly, and they are nearly the same set here.**

- **High-spend group** (external definition, disclosed): the five App Store markets
  consistently ranked largest by consumer spend — `us`, `jp`, `cn`, `gb`, `de`. This is an
  external industry ranking, **not** derived from this corpus.
- **High-review-volume group** (derived here, disclosed as a proxy): the five storefronts with
  the most *written reviews* in this corpus — `us`, `cn`, `gb`, `ca`, `de`. **This is review volume,
  not downloads**, and must not be read as a market-size proxy.

| | High-spend | High-volume |
|---|---:|---:|
| Reviews | 1,190 (66.67%) | 1,193 (66.83%) |
| Mean rating | 3.96★ | 3.96★ |
| `U:praise_any` | 65.88% | 66.55% |
| `U:bug_any` | 26.64% | 25.31% |
| `U:reliability` | 13.45% | 12.99% |
| `U:paywall_complaint` | 11.18% | 12.07% |
| `MON_PAID_EXPLICIT` | 10.84% | 11.23% |
| `BUG_WATCH` | 4.54% | 4.44% |
| `DEV_STALE` | 3.45% | 3.44% |
| `REQ_WIDGET` | 2.35% | 2.51% |

The two groups differ only in Japan vs Canada, and the aggregate profiles are almost identical.
**There is no finding here that is specific to high-spend markets** — the product's problems
are uniform across its commercially important geographies. That is itself worth knowing: there
is no market where this app is quietly working well.
